{'role': 'user', 'content': [{'type': 'text', 'text': '<file name="D:\\dev\\ab-tasks\\_briefs\\chessground\\brief.md">\n# Task: fix a reported bug in `chessground`\n\n## Bug report (from the project\'s issue tracker; reporter\'s proposed patch removed)\n**Disconnect ResizeObserver on destroy and on redraw**\n\n## Disconnect ResizeObserver on destroy and on redraw\n\n`bindBoard` constructs a `ResizeObserver` and observes `state.dom.elements.wrap`, but the observer is never stored or disconnected. This causes two distinct leaks:\n\n**Leak 1 — destroy:** `api.destroy()` calls `state.dom.unbind?.()`, which only unbinds the listeners registered by `bindDocument` (it returns the unbind function; `bindBoard` does not). The `ResizeObserver` is never disconnected, so the global RO controller continues to retain the wrap element — and through it, the entire detached board subtree.\n\n**Leak 2 — redraw:** `bindBoard` is called from inside `redrawAll`, which runs on every config change that rebuilds the DOM via `renderWrap`. Each call constructs a *new* RO observing the *new* wrap, but the *previous* RO is left observing the *previous* wrap (now detached). Within a single chessground lifetime, the observer count grows by one per full redraw. The previous RO\'s `onResize` callback also closes over the previous redraw\'s `elements`, so if it ever fires (e.g., the detached wrap is somehow resized), it operates on a partly-stale view of state.\n\n_(Reporter\'s proposed patch omitted.)_\n\n### Reproduction\n\n#### Reproduction — heap snapshot\n\n```html\n<!doctype html>\n<div id="root"></div>\n<script type="module">\nimport { Chessground } from \'@lichess-org/chessground\';\nwindow.test = () => {\n  for (let i = 0; i < 100; i++) {\n    const wrap = document.createElement(\'div\');\n    wrap.style.cssText = \'width:200px;height:200px\';\n    document.body.appendChild(wrap);\n    const cg = Chessground(wrap, { viewOnly: true });\n    cg.destroy();\n    wrap.remove();\n  }\n};\n</script>\n```\n\nRun `test()` from the console, take a heap snapshot, filter for `ResizeObserver` and `cg-board`. On redraw: `redrawAll` is called on every config change that rebuilds the DOM via `renderWrap` (`element.innerHTML = \'\'` creates a new board). `bindBoard` is called on line 47 of chessground.ts, creating a new RO each time. On destroy: `api.destroy()` calls `state.dom.unbind?.()`, but that\'s the unbind function from `bindDocument` — it doesn\'t disconnect the RO.\n\nAlso note: `bindBoard` adds event listeners on `boardEl` — on redraw, the old board element is detached. The listeners on the detached element die together with the element (the element gets GC\'d). But wait — the RO callback `onResize` closes over the old `elements`, and the RO retains the old wrap... the RO itself is retained by the ResizeObserver registry (an internal global map). So the old RO + old wrap + old board + old elements are all retained. This is the leak.\n\nHow does upstream lichess chessground fix this? Let me try to recall the actual upstream fix. Upstream chessground (lichess-org/chessground) — in the current version, `events.ts` has:\n\n```ts\nexport function bindBoard(s: State, onResize: () => void): cg.Unbind {\n  const boardEl = s.dom.elements.board;\n\n  if (\'ResizeObserver\' in window) {\n    const ro = new ResizeObserver(onResize);\n    ro.observe(s.dom.elements.wrap);\n    ...\n  }\n  ...\n}\n```\n\nHmm, let me think about the actual upstream code. The observed element (the wrap div) is retained by the controller. The wrap retains the entire board subtree. And the `onResize` callback retains `elements`/`state`. So everything leaks. Fix\n\n**`src/events.ts`**: `bindBoard` returns `cg.Unbind`:\n\n```ts\nexport function bindBoard(s: State, onResize: () => void): cg.Unbind {\n  const boardEl = s.dom.elements.board;\n  const unbinds: cg.Unbind[] = [];\n\n  if (\'ResizeObserver\' in window) {\n    const ro = new ResizeObserver(onResize);\n    ro.observe(s.dom.elements.wrap);\n    unbinds.push(() => ro.disconnect());\n  }\n\n  if (s.disableContextMenu || s.drawable.enabled) {\n    const onContextmenu = (e: Event) => e.preventDefault();\n    boardEl.addEventListener(\'contextmenu\', onContextmenu);\n    unbinds.push(() => boardEl.removeEventListener(\'contextmenu\', onContextmenu));\n  }\n\n  if (!s.viewOnly) {\n    const onStart = startDragOrDraw(s);\n    boardEl.addEventListener(\'touchstart\', onStart as EventListener, { passive: false });\n    boardEl.addEventListener(\'mousedown\', onStart as EventListener, { passive: false });\n    unbinds.push(() => {\n      boardEl.removeEventListener(\'touchstart\', onStart as EventListener);\n      boardEl.removeEventListener(\'mousedown\', onStart as EventListener);\n    });\n  }\n\n  return () => unbinds.forEach(f => f());\n}\n```\n\nHmm — should I bother unbinding the board-level listeners? They\'re attached to elements that get torn down and GC\'d on redraw/destroy. Unbinding them isn\'t necessary for the leak. But it is good hygiene, and it\'s consistent with the `bindDocument` style. However, "keep changes minimal and in project style." The bug report is specifically about the ResizeObserver. The minimal fix is to return an unbind from `bindBoard` that only disconnects the RO.\n\nActually, wait — let me reconsider. If I only disconnect the RO, the old board element\'s listeners still close over `s` — the same state object (no problem). And the old `elements` object is referenced by... the old `onResize` closure, which is referenced by the old RO. If the RO is disconnected, is the RO still alive? The RO\'s JS object becomes unreachable → GC\'d → the callback closure is freed → the old `elements` is freed → the old subtree is freed (assuming nothing else references it). So just disconnecting the RO fixes both leaks.\n\nBut is it correct/complete to only disconnect the RO? Yes, for the reported leaks. Keep it minimal: only handle the RO. Hmm, but there\'s one more consideration: consistency. If `bindBoard` returns an Unbind in the future that someone extends... The report is clearly about the RO. The minimal fix = just the RO. But there\'s an argument for removing the board listeners too: on redraw, `renderWrap` does `element.innerHTML = \'\'` — the old board element is detached, and its listeners die with it. No leak. But the old closure is discarded anyway.\n\nOne concern: `state.dom.unbind`\'s closure references the local variable `prevUnbind` — which is the value at that redraw (the first-draw document unbind, or undefined). Since `bindDocument` is only called when `!prevUnbind`, i.e., only on the first draw, all subsequent `prevUnbind` values are the same document unbind function. ✓\n\nBut — do we even need the `prevUnbind` local anymore? `\'dom\' in maybeState` check: on the first draw, `maybeState` has no `dom`, so `prevUnbind` is undefined. On later draws, `prevUnbind = maybeState.dom.unbind` = the composed closure from the previous draw — which calls `unbindBoard?.()` (the new one, possibly double-unbinding) and `prevUnbind` (document). Hmm! That\'s if I set `state.dom.unbind` to a composed closure and then on the next draw set `prevUnbind = state.dom.unbind` — the composed one. In my code above, `prevUnbind` is read from `state.dom.unbind` — the composed closure — and then I do `if (!prevUnbind) prevUnbind = events.bindDocument(...)`. On the 2nd draw, `prevUnbind` = composed closure (truthy), so bindDocument isn\'t called again ✓. And the new `state.dom.unbind` = `() => { unbindBoard?.(); prevUnbind?.(); }` where `prevUnbind` = the old composed closure = `() => { unbindBoard?.(); docUnbind?.(); }` — so calling the new one calls `unbindBoard` twice (once in the outer, once in the inner) — idempotent so harmless, but ugly: the unbind chain nests on each redraw (O(n²) call depth on destroy after n redraws… well, O(n) nesting, each calling the next).\n\nHmm, that\'s not clean. Let me restructure to avoid nesting: keep the document unbind in its own closure variable:\n\n```ts\nexport function Chessground(element: HTMLElement, config?: Config): Api {\n  const maybeState: State | HeadlessState = defaults();\n\n  configure(maybeState, config || {});\n\n  // the ResizeObserver and board listeners are re-bound on every redraw,\n  // so keep the unbind of the current generation to release the old one;\n  // document-wide listeners are only bound once.\n  let unbindBoard: cg.Unbind | undefined;\n  let unbindDocument: cg.Unbind | undefined;\n\n  function redrawAll(): State {\n    unbindBoard?.();\n    unbindBoard = undefined;\n    ...\n    const state = maybeState as State;\n    state.dom = {\n      elements,\n      bounds,\n      redraw: debounceRedraw(redrawNow),\n      redrawNow,\n      unbind: () => {\n        unbindBoard?.();\n        unbindDocument?.();\n      },\n    };\n    state.drawable.prevSvgHash = \'\';\n    updateBounds(state);\n    redrawNow(false);\n    unbindBoard = events.bindBoard(state, onResize);\n    unbindDocument ??= events.bindDocument(state, onResize);\n    state.events.insert?.(elements);\n    return state;\n  }\n\n  return start(redrawAll(), redrawAll);\n}\n```\n\nNow the `prevUnbind` local is no longer needed. `state.dom.unbind` is a flat closure: current board generation + document (bound only once). No nesting, no double-calls.\n\nEdge case — redraw after destroy: destroy sets `state.dom.destroyed = true`, calls unbind (unbinds current board + document). If user then calls `cg.redrawAll()`: `unbindBoard` is already undefined (called), `renderWrap` rebuilds, new board binding, `unbindDocument ??=` — already set so no rebind (same behavior as before: document listeners stay unbound; previous code also didn\'t rebind. Previous code: `if (!prevUnbind)` — prevUnbind existed → no rebind. Same). OK, behavior on this edge case is unchanged.\n\nType of `state.dom.unbind` — `Unbind | undefined`; we always assign a function now. Fine.\n\nNow, does `api.ts`\'s `start` need changes? `destroy()` calls `state.dom.unbind?.()` — that now includes the RO disconnect. ✓ No changes needed.\n\nWait, one more consideration — the `Dom` interface has `unbind?: Unbind`. In `redrawAll`, before my change, the first draw assigned `state.dom = {..., unbind: prevUnbind}` (undefined), then `state.dom.unbind = events.bindDocument(...)`. Same shape.\n\nAlso double-check that no other code reads `state.dom.unbind`… grep for `.unbind` in src.\n\nAlso check: any other references to `ResizeObserver` — only events.ts. ✓\n\nNow — the test file. The mock constructor takes a callback; we could save it and call it manually. A stronger test for leak 2: after a redraw, firing the *old* observer\'s callback should not operate on the state… but that\'s hard to assert meaningfully (it would call updateBounds/renderResized on the shared state — no observable difference).
